# Lab genealogy: who went where in frontier AI labs, 2010–2026

Generated from `pack.json` by `node research/lab-genealogy/tools.mjs render`; do not edit by hand. A candidate research pack outside application data: machine-prepared and machine-audited, with no human review. Each line is one record; read its source before relying on it.

## How to read this

- A line records that a source **documents** an event or a role at a date. It does not say why anything happened. Where a person or organisation stated a reason in its own words, the record attributes it (see [Stated reasons](#stated-reasons)).
- Dates keep their precision. "as of" marks a role shown on a dated document (a snapshot), not a start date. An announcement date is not a start or last working day. "Undated" means no source gave a date.
- "(reporting)" marks a record that rests on news reporting because no primary source could be retrieved.
- `dataset record` lines are already in Selekoa's dataset and are shown here for continuity; they were not re-checked by this pack.
- A sequence of roles is not a continuous employment history, and a move is not a transfer of influence, ideas or loyalty.

## Coverage

477 new candidate records from 232 sources (173 primary, 59 reporting), shown together with 129 existing dataset records on the same labs. 221 people appear in the pack (183 proposed as new entities) and 53 organisations are proposed as new entities. 13 records carry a reason stated by the person or organisation concerned.

## Lab by lab

### Google: DeepMind, Google Brain and Google DeepMind

DeepMind, Google Brain and Google DeepMind stay distinct entities; the 2023 combination is recorded as a combination, not a legal merger of companies. Google and Alphabet are grouped here as the parent organisations, so a move between Google and one of its research units is not shown as a move between labs.

OpenAI (executive roles) is kept apart from OpenAI, Inc. / OpenAI Foundation (nonprofit board) and OpenAI Group PBC, as in the dataset.

| 11 March 2019         | OpenAI → OpenAI LP                                                      | OpenAI announced on 11 March 2019 that it was creating OpenAI LP as a hybrid of a for-profit and a nonprofit, which it called a “capped-profit” company. Investors and employees could receive a capped return, with the first round of investors capped at 100 times their investment, and returns beyond the cap would go to OpenAI Nonprofit. | dataset record                              | `cs-openai-lp-announced-2019`                 |
| 2023                  | OpenAI LP → OpenAI OpCo, LLC                                            | OpenAI, L.P. became OpenAI OpCo, L.L.C. in 2023. ## Timeline

### 2005

- **August 2005–December 2020.** Zhipu’s Hong Kong prospectus of 30 December 2025 states that Zhang Peng worked at Tsinghua University from August 2005 to December 2020. _(Knowledge Atlas Technology Joint Stock Company Limited (via HKEXnews); `lg-cn-zhang-peng-tsinghua-2005-2020`)_

### 2008

- **December 2008– (end not stated).** Zhipu’s Hong Kong prospectus of 30 December 2025 states that Li Juanzi has been a tenured professor in Tsinghua University’s Department of Computer Science and Technology since December 2008. _(Knowledge Atlas Technology Joint Stock Company Limited (via HKEXnews); `lg-cn-li-juanzi-tsinghua-professor-2008`)_

### 2010

- **2010.** Demis Hassabis co-founded DeepMind in 2010. _(dataset record; `demis-hassabis-cofounded-deepmind-2010`)_
- **2010.** Google DeepMind's organisation page states that DeepMind started in 2010; Demis Hassabis's message of 20 April 2023 also dates DeepMind's launch to 2010. _(Google DeepMind; Google DeepMind; `lg-goo-deepmind-founding-stated-2010`)_
- **2010.** Mustafa Suleyman co-founded DeepMind in 2010. _(dataset record; `mustafa-suleyman-cofounded-deepmind-2010`)_
- **2010.** Shane Legg co-founded DeepMind in 2010. _(dataset record; `shane-legg-cofounded-deepmind-2010`)_

### 2011

- **as of 2011.** Andrej Karpathy’s biography places an internship at Google Brain in 2011. _(dataset record; `karpathy-brain-intern-2011`)_
- **2011.** Google DeepMind's organisation page states that Google Brain started in 2011 at X, the moonshot factory. _(Google DeepMind; `lg-goo-brain-founding-stated-2011`)_
- **as of 2011.** Google DeepMind's organisation page states that Google Brain started at X, the moonshot factory, in 2011. _(Google DeepMind; `lg-goo-brain-unit-of-x-2011`)_
- **2011–2012.** Andrew Ng served as founding lead of the Google Brain project from 2011 to 2012. _(dataset record; `ng-brain-lead-2011-2012`)_

### 2012

- **as of 26 June 2012.** Google’s blog post of 26 June 2012 by Jeff Dean and Andrew Ng states that the large-scale neural-network experiments it reports were led by Quoc Le. _(Google (The Keyword / Official Google Blog); `lg-gw-le-google-experiments-2012`)_

### 2013

- **2013.** Meta's FAIR ten-year retrospective of 30 November 2023 dates the launch of FAIR to late 2013. _(Meta AI; `lg-mx-fair-founding-2013`)_
- **December 2013– (end not stated).** Facebook’s historical biography dates LeCun’s AI research directorship to December 2013. _(dataset record; `xm-lecun-fair-start`)_

### 2014

- **2014.** Google acquired DeepMind in early 2014. _(dataset record; `google-acquired-deepmind-2014`)_

### 2015

- **2015.** OpenAI’s launch announcement names Durk Kingma among its founding members. _(dataset record; `durk-kingma-openai-founding-2015`)_

### 2023

### Grouped events

Records that belong to one announced event, as grouped by the preparer.

- **26 June 2012 — Google blog post by Jeff Dean and Andrew Ng reports large-scale neural-network experiments led by Quoc Le:** `lg-gw-le-google-experiments-2012`
- **2013 — Facebook launches FAIR (late 2013, as Meta later dated it):** `lg-mx-fair-founding-2013`
- **11 December 2015 — OpenAI launches as a nonprofit research company:** `lg-oai-andrej-karpathy-founding-member-2015`, `lg-oai-founding-stated-2015`, `lg-oai-pamela-vagata-founding-member-2015`, `lg-oai-trevor-blackwell-founding-member-2015`, `lg-oai-vicki-cheung-founding-member-2015`

- **2017 — AI21 Labs founded (as reported):** `lg-new-ai21-founding-2017`, `lg-new-goshen-ai21-cofounder`, `lg-new-shashua-ai21-cofounder`, `lg-new-shoham-ai21-cofounder`
- **11 April 2018 — DeepMind announces Lila Ibrahim as its first COO:** `lg-goo-ibrahim-deepmind-coo-2018`
- **2019 — Cohere founded in Toronto (as stated by Cohere):** `lg-new-cohere-founding-2019`, `lg-new-frosst-cohere-cofounder`, `lg-new-gomez-cohere-cofounder`, `lg-new-zhang-cohere-cofounder`

## Who went where

People documented at two or more organisations in this pack and the dataset, in date order. Each step is a separate record; gaps between them are unknown, undated roles are listed last, and the path is not an employment history.

- **Daniela Amodei:** co-founded Anthropic, 2021 `daniela-cofounded-anthropic-2021` → Anthropic (President), as of 28 May 2021 `daniela-anthropic-2021` → Anthropic (Chair of the board of directors), as of 15 October 2026 `lg-ant-daniela-board-chair-2026`; undated: OpenAI (VP of Safety), undated `daniela-openai-safety-prior`
- **Dario Amodei:** departure announced from OpenAI (VP of Research), 29 December 2020 `dario-openai-departure-announced-2020` → OpenAI (VP of Research), as of 29 December 2020 `dario-openai-vp-2020` → co-founded Anthropic, 2021 `dario-cofounded-anthropic-2021` → Anthropic (CEO), as of 28 May 2021 `dario-anthropic-2021` → Anthropic (Chief Executive Officer), as of 15 October 2026 `lg-ant-dario-ceo-2026`

For each person above, each change of organisation between consecutive dated records (a lab and its units count as one organisation), with the two records. The order is that of the dates of the records, which may be snapshots or announcements: a pair is not a documented transfer, it does not say the earlier role had ended, and undated records, departure announcements and changes between records whose dates overlap (the same day, or 2024 and May 2024) are left out. ## Stated reasons

Reasons appear only where the person or organisation concerned stated them in a cited source. They are attributed statements, not findings.

- `lg-ant-matheny-ltbt-departure-2023` — Anthropic stated: Anthropic's page states that he stepped down to preempt potential conflicts of interest with RAND Corporation's policy-related initiatives. _(evidence `lg-ev-ant-matheny-ltbt-departure-2023`)_
- `lg-gs-kokotajlo-openai-departure-2024` — Daniel Kokotajlo stated: Kokotajlo states that he quit because he had lost confidence that OpenAI would behave responsibly around the time of AGI. _(evidence `lg-ev-gs-kokotajlo-openai-departure-2024-profile`)_
- `lg-oai-leike-departure-2024` — Jan Leike stated: Leike stated that he had been disagreeing with OpenAI leadership about the company’s core priorities for quite some time, until a breaking point was reached. _(evidence `lg-ev-oai-leike-departure-2024-2`)_
- `lg-ant-muehlhauser-board-departure-2024` — Anthropic stated: Anthropic's announcement states that he stepped down to focus on his work at Open Philanthropy. _(evidence `lg-ev-ant-muehlhauser-board-departure-2024`)_
- `lg-deal-amazon-adept-agreement-2024` — Adept stated: Adept stated that continuing its initial plan of building both general intelligence and an enterprise agent product would have required significant attention on fundraising for its foundation models, and presented the strategy updates as a way to keep momentum on its agent technology. _(evidence `lg-ev-deal-amazon-adept-agreement-reason`)_
- `lg-oai-schulman-departure-2024` — John Schulman stated: Schulman stated that the choice stemmed from his wish to deepen his focus on AI alignment and to return to hands-on technical work. _(evidence `lg-ev-oai-schulman-departure-2024`)_
- `lg-gs-brundage-openai-departure-2024` — Miles Brundage stated: Brundage stated that he wanted to impact and influence AI's development from outside the industry rather than inside. _(evidence `lg-ev-gs-brundage-openai-departure-2024-reason`)_
- `lg-gs-campbell-openai-departure-2024` — Rosie Campbell stated: Campbell stated that after Brundage's departure and the dissolution of the AGI Readiness team she believed she could pursue the mission of safe and beneficial AGI more effectively outside OpenAI. _(evidence `lg-ev-gs-campbell-openai-departure-2024-reason`)_
- `lg-ant-bahl-ltbt-departure-2026` — Anthropic stated: Anthropic's page states that she stepped down to begin a new nonprofit, the AI Access Initiative. _(evidence `lg-ev-ant-bahl-ltbt-departure-2026`)_
- `lg-ant-robinson-ltbt-departure-2026` — Anthropic stated: Anthropic's page states that he stepped down to focus on non-profit and philanthropic work. _(evidence `lg-ev-ant-robinson-ltbt-departure-2026`)_
- `lg-oai-weil-departure-2026` — Kevin Weil stated: Weil wrote that his last day came as OpenAI for Science was being decentralized into other research teams. _(evidence `lg-ev-oai-weil-departure-2026`)_
- `lg-ant-cuellar-ltbt-departure-2026` — Anthropic stated: Anthropic's announcement states that he stepped down from the Trust to join the company. _(evidence `lg-ev-ant-cuellar-ltbt-departure-2026`)_
- `lg-goo-hassabis-gdm-chair-2026` — Demis Hassabis stated: Hassabis wrote that he was handing over day-to-day operational responsibilities so that he would have time and space to focus on the big picture. _(evidence `lg-ev-goo-hassabis-handover-2026`)_
